# Library map

Generated by `tools/progress/gen.py` from `tools/progress/data/` (refresh with `tools/progress/sync.py`). Upstream: TomBox `7e8064f`; emulator source paths link to upstream. Titles analysed: Fortnite (console OS 10.0.19041.3948), XDefiant (console OS 10.0.22000.4429), Terraria (console OS 10.0.22000.4429), Trackmania (console OS 10.0.22621.1852).

## Scope: Xbox console libraries only

The map counts only what the console provides and a PC does not. A console export is console-related when this PC's `C:\Windows\System32` has no DLL of the same name, or has one that does not export that name (for an export by ordinal only: that ordinal). A console DLL that is a modified PC DLL (kernel32, advapi32, mfplat, wldp, ...) therefore counts only for the exports the PC DLL lacks; console DLLs whose every export exists in the PC DLL of the same name (ntdll, msvcrt, ucrtbase, combase, xgameruntime, GameInput, ...) are left out. Title imports are counted the same way: imports that a PC System32 DLL or PC API set provides, and imports from DLLs shipped with the title, are excluded from both done and total. Absence from System32 is the only test, so a few non-Xbox components that happen not to ship with desktop Windows (symsrv, ConIoSrv, KernelTraceControl, the Media Foundation H.264/HEVC decoder DLLs) are counted.

## Statuses

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| implemented | the emulator provides its own implementation |
| forwarded | an emulator DLL exports it and forwards to a PC system component (D3D12, Media Foundation, DirectStorage) |
| stub | the emulator exports it but returns a fixed result (E_NOTIMPL / S_OK / inert object) |
| unverified | imported by a binary or module that was never observed loading |
| missing | no emulator export, and no PC System32 export of that name |

Shader compile: all 139 shaders of the lobby frame compile with DXC (TomBox `docs/DEVELOPMENT_LOG.md:894`); 25/25 fixtures compile (`:672`). The full-library compile sweep (`:706`) has no published final count, so no compile badge is shown.

Direct3D 12.x: 160 / 128 are the table sizes the wrappers allocate (`d3d12_xs/src/device_wrap.rs`, `cmdlist_wrap.rs`), not documented interface sizes. Device: done = forwarded PC slots 0-43 plus intercepted Xbox slots 44, 48, 52 (SetDebugFlags_X, documented no-op), 55, 56/57 and 66/67 (graphics/compute PSO serialize/deserialize, emulator TBPS format only), 60-62, 73, 78-81, 87 (OpenExistingHeapFromAddress via ID3D12Device3); partial = logging S_OK stubs in 45-90, including 74 (RegisterPagePoolX, bookkeeping only). CommandList: done = 0-26, 32-52 (PC methods shifted by 4), 59, 67, 68, 83; 27-31 are Xbox-inserted stubs and 53-127 log-and-ignore probes, counted as not done.

Direct3D 12.x on Xbox One (Terraria, Trackmania; `crates/compat/d3d12_x`): the console `d3d12_x.dll` is a 48 KB loader (export table identical in both dumps, 54 exports, ordinals 1-54) that loads a user-mode driver flavour (`xgs12.dll`, `xgs12_d.dll`, `xgs12_i.dll`) and calls its `UMDCreateDevice12`; Scarlett's `d3d12_xs.dll` loads `xgs12_10*.dll` instead. The COM vtables live in those drivers, not in `d3d12_x.dll`. Neither driver carries RTTI type names for the D3D12 classes. Tables found statically (a constructor `lea` of an `.rdata` table of code pointers, inside a `.pdata` function) number 109 in `xgs12.dll` and 67 in `xgs12_10.dll`; the only one tied to an interface IID through its QueryInterface is ID3D12CommandQueue: 34 slots on Xbox One, 35 on Scarlett. The ID3D12Device and ID3D12GraphicsCommandList tables were not identified by static analysis: both drivers hold long unattributed runs of static code pointers (about 480 entries in `xgs12.dll`, 520 in `xgs12_10.dll`) that may contain them, or the tables may be assembled at run time. So the Xbox One layout of the Xbox-specific slots (Device 44+, CommandList 27-31 and 53+) is unverified. From the titles' own call sites (device pointer traced from the `D3D12XboxCreateDevice` output: Trackmania `[renderer+0x3e80]`, Terraria a global; then `mov rax,[dev]; call [rax+8*n]`), the device slots the titles call are: Trackmania 2, 13, 15, 18-20, 52, 53, 59, 80; Terraria 0, 2, 9, 10, 13-16, 18, 19, 22, 25, 27-30, 36-38, 41, 48, 62, 64, 65, 68, 69, 73, 79-81. Xbox-range slots used by an Xbox One title are therefore 48, 52, 53, 59, 62, 64, 65, 68, 69, 73, 79, 80, 81; their meaning on Xbox One is not yet matched to the Scarlett slots of the same index (Trackmania calls 53 with no arguments and feeds its result to 52 right after creation). Both titles fill the first dword of the 0x28-byte create-parameter block with 0x1000a. The interface IIDs are unchanged (`IID_ID3D12Device` 189819f1-..., the PC GUID), the `D3D12XboxCreateDevice` parameter block is 0x28 bytes and NULL is rejected with E_INVALIDARG. The emulator therefore forwards Xbox One device creation to `d3d12_xs.dll` (Scarlett wrapper): PC slots 0-43 behave as on PC; any Xbox-only slot a title calls goes to the Scarlett slot of the same index, which may be a different method. `D3DMapEsramMemory(Flags, VA, NumPages, PageList)` (signature from the console stub, 0x18-byte driver packet) accepts flags 1 and 3 only and is mapped onto ordinary committed memory of NumPages x 64 KiB at the caller's VA.

## Terraria imports (4.4.14.1081)

Unity 2021.3.16f1 GameCore **Xbox One** player (`TargetDeviceFamily="XboxOne"`, PDB `GameCoreXboxOnePlayerNoDevelopment_Master`), IL2CPP scripting backend (no CLR header; C# compiled to `Data/Native/GameAssembly.dll` + `global-metadata.dat`), FMOD audio over XAudio2, Unity `baselib.dll`. Scanned: `Mount` (Terraria.exe, XCurl, baselib, MSVC 14.28 CRT), `Mount/Data/Native` (GameAssembly + MSVC 14.29 CRT), `Mount/Data/Plugins` (PartyWin, PartyXboxLive, PlayFabMultiplayerGDK, TerrariaNative, XCurl). Imports `d3d12_x.dll` / `xg_x.dll` / `XFrontPanelDisplay.dll` (the Xbox One variants, not the Scarlett `_xs` ones); the emulator provides them as separate crates with the console's export ordinals, and the remaining gaps are stubs. XGameRuntime is loaded by name (`xgameruntime.dll` + `QueryApiImpl`), so its services are not in the import table.

Static analysis only: this title has not been run.

## Trackmania imports (1.6.2.0)

Nadeo GBX engine (ManiaPlanet lineage), native C++ GameCore **Xbox One** build (`TargetDeviceFamily="XboxOne"`, PDB folder `Release_Gaming.Xbox.XboxOne.x64`, build id `2024-04-30_c8c94a9ed_..._XB1`), GDK edition string `October 2022 GRDK` in the EXE, Ubisoft UbiServices SDK 2020.Release.62. Scanned: `Mount` (GbxGame.exe, GameChat2.dll 2210, XCurl.dll 2210, MSVC 14.29 CRT) and `Mount/aegir` (aegir.dll, 36 MB, a WebKit/JavaScriptCore UI engine: 12,887 `WebCore`/`JSC` exports). GameChat2.dll and aegir.dll are not in GbxGame.exe's import table; they are loaded at run time (the EXE carries `aegir.dll` loader strings). Imports `d3d12_x.dll` / `xg_x.dll` / `dxcompiler_x.dll` (Xbox One variants, not the Scarlett `_xs` ones); the emulator provides them as separate crates with the console's export ordinals, and the remaining gaps are stubs. XGameRuntime is loaded by name.

## Xbox console libraries (EmbeddedXvd, union of all analysed titles)

Console-related exports of the DLLs in each title's EmbeddedXvd `Windows\System32` and `System32\forwarders` (union, so the newest console OS defines the surface; see Scope: exports a same-named PC System32 DLL also has are not counted), plus the GDK wrappers the emulator redirects inside the Fortnite EXE (statically linked, not exports; build-specific). The emulator's `mfplat.dll` proxy shadows the PC one for the title, so console mfplat exports it does not forward are counted missing. xgameruntime.dll exports only its dispatcher entry points; the GDK services behind it (XGameSave, XGameUi, XNetworking, ...) are reached via `QueryApiImpl` and are not enumerated here; its exports all exist in the PC xgameruntime.dll, so it is not counted. Import attribution for API-set imports picks one host DLL (KernelBase, ucrtbase, ntdll, combase, ... first).

### Not targeted (no analysed title references them)

The console-library total above (1901 exports in 89 libraries) keeps every console-only export in the denominator, including devkit, tools and OS service DLLs that no analysed title imports. 19 libraries (1009 exports) are imported by at least one title, directly or through API-set host attribution, or are emulator-provided; the 70 below (892 exports) are not targeted: no title EXE or shipped DLL imports any of their exports and the emulator does not provide them. They stay `missing` in the headline instead of being dropped from it, so the percentage is not inflated; a library moves out of this list as soon as a title imports it or an emulator crate exports it. The earlier Xbox-only figure (1824 exports) counted only DLLs with no same-named PC DLL and is kept in `summary.json` under `all_including_pc`. Coverage inside the 19 targeted libraries alone: 179/1009.

### Evidence files

`sync.py` copies these from TomBox `analysis/progress_evidence/` into `tools/progress/data/evidence/`; `gen.py` reads them, and a missing file changes nothing. Schema: `{"title": "Fortnite", "source": "<log or test>", "modules": {"<Module.dll>": {"loaded": true, "resolved": ["<export>", ...], "unresolved": []}}}`. A module counts as loading and resolving only when `loaded` is true and `unresolved` is an empty list; a system-functions import flips to done only when its name is in `resolved`. `oshost.json` is `{"file": "<crate path>", "hooks": {"<console or PC twin DLL>": ["<export>", ...]}}`.

| File | Used for | Present |
|---|---|---|
| `chatprobe.json` | Fortnite GameChat2.dll / CONCRT140.dll leave the unverified lists; GameChat2 delay import in system functions | no |
| `media.json` | MFPlat.DLL x16 and MFReadWrite.dll x2 in system functions | no |
| `gameinput_ext.json` | GameInput.dll imports outside the EXE (TerrariaNative.dll) | no |
| `oshost.json` | PC-twin rows become `hooked` instead of `pc` | no |

### Exports not present in every title's console OS

Newer console OS builds add exports; each is its own item in the map and stays `missing` until implemented.

## Verified on this machine (Fortnite)

| Milestone | Evidence |
|---|---|
| Boots (process starts, GDK runtime services up) | `%TEMP%\tombox\run.txt:1-4` |
| First frames (PresentX into the title window) | `%TEMP%\tombox\tombox_compat.log:14540` |
| Window clamped to the primary monitor | `%TEMP%\tombox\tombox_compat.log:12987` |
| Login complete (HandleLoginRequestComplete Result=2) | `%TEMP%\tombox\run.txt:5962` |
| Hotfix check UpdateSuccess | `%TEMP%\tombox\run.txt:3401,6094` |
| Game-mode select (UI.State.Startup.SubgameSelect) | `%TEMP%\tombox\run.txt:6249` |
| Battle Royale lobby (UI.State.Athena.Frontend) | `%TEMP%\tombox\run.txt:6496` |

| Audio | not reached |
| Gameplay | not reached |

## Unverified

- Fortnite: GameChat2.dll (14 delay imports) and everything it imports: never seen loading.
- Fortnite: CONCRT140.dll (MSVCP140 delay import): not observed.
- GameInput.dll ordinal imports: #1: console GameInput.dll ordinal 1 = GameInputCreate on 19041.3948 / 22000.4429 / 22621.1852 (pefile).
- XAudio2_9 ordinal imports are treated as PC-provided because the PC DLL exports those ordinals; that the console DLL uses the same ordinals was not checked here. XAudio2 starts streaming in the Fortnite log, but there is no audio.
- PC-twin rows (minuserx -> user32, kernel32legacy -> kernel32 / kernelbase, advapi32legacy -> advapi32, minappmodelx -> twinapi.appcore / kernel.appcore) are export-name matches on the twin only; the console DLLs' own behaviour is not reproduced.
- PC-provided is an export-name match (ordinal match for ordinal-only exports) against this PC's System32; behaviour differences between console and PC system DLLs are not measured, and such exports are outside this map.
- Titles without run evidence are classified statically only.
- Milestones 14/19 and the RDNA2 instruction table are upstream's numbers (`progress.json`, `data/mnemonics.txt`); not re-measured here.
